# Restore feed readme

## 1. What a feed is

A restore feed is the set of files in one directory whose control block names the feed identifier of the restore request. Files are identified only by their content: the format by its leading bytes and structure, the role by the stream, part and status of its control block. File names and extensions carry no meaning and must never decide anything, because feeds are re-delivered under different names.

## 2. Control blocks

Every feed file except the project archive carries one control block: space-separated key=value pairs whose values never contain spaces. Keys always present: feed, stream, part, of, status, records, custody. Other keys (locale, revision, effective_from, source_custody, note) appear where a stream needs them. Locations: the first line '#control ...' of delimited, fixed-width and log files; the 'control' object of JSON documents and of the first JSONL line; the control element of XML; the 'restore-control' key of the Parquet schema metadata; the _control sheet of workbooks and the _control table of SQLite databases (key, value rows); the control mapping, table or section of YAML, TOML and INI files; the X-RESTORE-CONTROL property of the calendar; the X-Restore-Control header of the first message of the mailbox (a control message that is not a record); the first line 'CONTROL ...' of PDF and Word documents; the restore-control comment of Markdown and the restore-control meta element of HTML.

## 3. Records

records states the number of data records of the file: data rows of tables and sheets (headers, schema lines, comments and control rows excluded), objects of JSON arrays and JSONL lines, record elements of XML, log lines after the control line, calendar events, mailbox messages after the control message, items of YAML and TOML lists, sections of INI files (for the local account register: account lines), and rows of all tables of a document.

## 4. Parts, truncation and resends

of states how many ORIGINAL parts the stream has; the feed registry below repeats the part counts of each feed. A part that holds fewer records than its control block states lost its tail in transfer; the lost tail is re-published as a RESEND part with the same stream and part. A RESEND part is of record only for entries whose entry identifier does not occur in the ORIGINAL part; for every other entry the ORIGINAL part wins.

## 5. Corrected copies

A CORRECTED copy of a part is produced by a help-desk script and is never a catalog source. When the custody value of an ORIGINAL part is N/A, the custody digest uses the source_custody value of the CORRECTED copy of that part instead.

## 6. Custody digest

For every file of the feed, whatever its status, form the line stream|part|status|records|custody from its control block (values as written, custody replaced as in section 5). Sort the lines by Unicode code points, terminate each with a line feed, and take the SHA-256 of the UTF-8 bytes. The lowercase hexadecimal digest is the custody digest every deliverable carries.

## Feed registry

parts lists, per stream, how many ORIGINAL parts the feed delivers.
